    ↓\n"},{"number":1106,"text":"                                                                                Gap Analysis\n"},{"number":1107,"text":" environment;\n"},{"number":1118,"text":"  code is the software case, and a performed human procedure is another case.\n"},{"number":1119,"text":"- **Observations** establish what occurred and what Apply\n"},{"number":1227,"text":"[Contract application](TRANSFORMATION_LIBRARY.md#contract-application) to the\n"},{"number":1228,"text":"affected claim. This family chooses mandatory detection, lifecycle sufficiency\n"},{"number":1229,"text":"and the hard stops below; the Product's declared risk policy separately chooses\n"},{"number":1230,"text":"which remaining ambiguities may be carried, resolved or escalated.\n"},{"number":1231,"text":"\n"},{"number":1232,"text":"`SPEC_METHOD.md` owns the substrate-neutral ambiguity-governance bounded\n"},{"number":1233,"text":"context `urn:stdo:bounded-context:ambiguity-governance` under the selected\n"},{"number":1234,"text":"complete STDO basis. Graph/runtime shorthands such as `F_D`, `F_P`, and `F_H`\n"},{"number":1235,"text":"remain in the graph-native ODD context unless an explicit relation imports or\n"},{"number":1236,"text":"translates them.\n"},{"number":1237,"text":"\n"},{"number":1238,"text":"Spec-driven development is not only a derivation pipeline. It is a governed\n"},{"number":1239,"text":"disambiguation pipeline.\n"},{"number":1240,"text":"\n"},{"number":1241,"text":"The point of the upstream chain is to progressively reduce ambiguity:\n"},{"number":1242,"text":"\n"},{"number":1243,"text":"- goals narrow active priority and work-wave focus\n"},{"number":1244,"text":"- intent narrows direction and scope\n"},{"number":1245,"text":"- product narrows current realization shape and terms\n"},{"number":1246,"text":"- requirements narrow constitutional obligation\n"},{"number":1247,"text":"- build-tenant or stack choice narrows executable realization class\n"},{"number":1248,"text":"- design narrows structural interpretation\n"},{"number":1249,"text":"- implementation narrows local realization detail\n"},{"number":1250,"text":"\n"},{"number":1251,"text":"This narrowing is not uniform. The methodology distinguishes between:\n"},{"number":1252,"text":"\n"},{"number":1253,"text":"- **major ambiguity**: ambiguity that materially changes architecture, stack,\n"},{"number":1254,"text":"  product boundary, execution/deployment admissibility, public contract shape,\n"},{"number":1255,"text":"  or other downstream realization law\n"},{"number":1256,"text":"- **micro ambiguity**: local implementation choice that remains inside an\n"},{"number":1257,"text":"  already-governed design boundary\n"},{"number":1258,"text":"\n"},{"number":1259,"text":"Major ambiguity must always be surfaced and governed. It may not remain hidden\n"},{"number":1260,"text":"inside informal operator judgment, ambient precedent, or silent model choice.\n"},{"number":1261,"text":"\n"},{"number":1262,"text":"Therefore, at each major boundary the process must:\n"},{"number":1263,"text":"\n"},{"number":1264,"text":"- detect and record the major ambiguity that remains or is newly introduced\n"},{"number":1265,"text":"- identify the affected invariant, asset, or decision boundary\n"},{"number":1266,"text":"- record the decision taken if work proceeds\n"},{"number":1267,"text":"- record whether the ambiguity was resolved, carried forward, escalated, or\n"},{"number":1268,"text":"  blocked\n"},{"number":1269,"text":"\n"},{"number":1270,"text":"Ambiguity detection is mandatory. Blocking is policy.\n"},{"number":1271,"text":"\n"},{"number":1272,"text":"**Lawful Probabilistic Processing**\n"},{"number":1273,"text":"(`urn:stdo:concept:ambiguity-governance:lawful-probabilistic-processing`) is\n"},{"number":1274,"text":"bounded non-human processing permitted by declared policy to carry or resolve\n"},{"number":1275,"text":"declared ambiguity. **Human Adjudication**\n"},{"number":1276,"text":"(`urn:stdo:concept:ambiguity-governance:human-adjudication`) is explicit human\n"},{"number":1277,"text":"judgment admitted by authority to resolve declared ambiguity where policy or\n"},{"number":1278,"text":"risk appetite requires it.\n"},{"number":1279,"text":"\n"},{"number":1280,"text":"The default governance model is:\n"},{"number":1281,"text":"\n"},{"number":1282,"text":"- ambiguity may be carried or decided by lawful probabilistic processing when\n"},{"number":1283,"text":"  project policy allows it\n"},{"number":1284,"text":"- ambiguity may be escalated to explicit human adjudication when project policy\n"},{"number":1285,"text":"  requires it\n"},{"number":1286,"text":"- the threshold between those actions is determined by declared risk appetite,\n"},{"number":1287,"text":"  not by silent convenience\n"},{"number":1288,"text":"\n"},{"number":1289,"text":"Projects may therefore choose different ambiguity-handling policies. A lower\n"},{"number":1290,"text":"risk appetite escalates more major ambiguity to explicit human judgment. A\n"},{"number":1291,"text":"higher risk appetite permits more bounded probabilistic decision-making. In either\n"},{"number":1292,"text":"case, the ambiguity and the decision must be recorded.\n"},{"number":1293,"text":"\n"},{"number":1294,"text":"`Resolved`, `carried forward`, `escalated` and `blocked` record handling, not four\n"},{"number":1295,"text":"verdicts on the affected Product claim. Evaluate `the identified ambiguity is\n"},{"number":1296,"text":"resolved under its governing meaning`: established resolution is `satisfied`,\n"},{"number":1297,"text":"an established remaining ambiguity is `falsified`, and insufficient evidence is\n"},{"number":1298,"text":"`indeterminate`, retaining applicable `invalid_basis` or `out_of_frame`\n"},{"number":1299,"text":"diagnoses. A carried or escalated ambiguity may still be unresolved. Separately,\n"},{"number":1300,"text":"the core decision predicate evaluates whether the recorded handling was made\n"},{"number":1301,"text":"under the bound policy and authority: supported lawful handling is `satisfied`,\n"},{"number":1302,"text":"a demonstrated violation is `falsified`, and unsupported handling is\n"},{"number":1303,"text":"`indeterminate`, subject to the same basis/scope diagnoses. Neither result\n"},{"number":1304,"text":"settles delivery or grants authority outside that policy.\n"},{"number":1305,"text":"\n"},{"number":1306,"text":"Some conditions are not optional ambiguity decisions and therefore remain hard\n"},{"number":1307,"text":"stops regardless of risk appetite. Typical hard-stop classes include:\n"},{"number":1308,"text":"\n"},{"number":1309,"text":"- violated invariant or guarantee\n"},{"number":1310,"text":"- absent required authority surface\n"},{"number":1311,"text":"- missing declared capability for an executional or operational stage\n"},{"number":1312,"text":"- undeclared irreversible side effect\n"},{"number":1313,"text":"- explicit policy gate requiring human approval\n"},{"number":1314,"text":"\n"},{"number":1315,"text":"A hard stop returns the affected task predicate through the\n"},{"number":1316,"text":"[core stop rule](FOUNDATIONS.md#result-algebra). A witnessed invariant violation\n"},{"number":1317,"text":"or unmet required authorization falsifies the corresponding admissibility\n"},{"number":1318,"text":"predicate; missing evidence about that condition leaves it indeterminate.\n"},{"number":1319,"text":"Admissibility and fulfillment of the interrupted task remain separate\n"},{"number":1320,"text":"predicates: refusal alone does not establish that the intended outcome failed.\n"},{"number":1321,"text":"An unusable governing\n"},{"number":1322,"text":"basis or an evaluation outside capability retains its own diagnosis. The stop\n"},{"number":1323,"text":"label proves neither non-performance nor absence of effects; an undeclared or\n"},{"number":1324,"text":"uncertain effect needs actual observation before dependent action.\n"},{"number":1325,"text":"\n"},{"number":1326,"text":"The methodology is therefore not \"eliminate all ambiguity before work.\" It is\n"},{"number":1327,"text":"\"make ambiguity visible, govern it explicitly, and reduce it progressively until\n"},{"number":1328,"text":"downstream realization is sufficiently constrained.\"\n"},{"number":1329,"text":"\n"},{"number":1330,"text":"---\n"},{"number":1331,"text":"\n"}]},{"end_line":1380,"heading":"Spec-Driven Homeostatic Methodology/Operational Lifecycle Sufficiency Rule","id":"repo://stdo-v3-generation-rc9/codex/5592168e26759f37e730aed12c030a6061709ec4/source/standards/SPEC_METHOD.md#Spec-Driven%20Homeostatic%20Methodology%2FOperational%20Lifecycle%20Sufficiency%20Rule","path":"standards/SPEC_METHOD.md","sha256":"d8b16f8d7708e5a0ea2dccf3898d1a2774e4a63ba1ae6a148b63cbd53e0bee5c","start_line":1332,"source_file":"/Users/jim/src/apps/specification_methodology/a_c/dogfood/stdo-v3-generation-rc9/runs-v2/codex/graphify-ac/corpus/standards/SPEC_METHOD.md","lines":[{"number":1332,"text":"## Operational Lifecycle Sufficiency Rule\n"},{"number":1333,"text":"\n"},{"number":1334,"text":"Constitutional specification must treat a designed thing as more than its build\n"},{"number":1335,"text":"shape.\n"},{"number":1336,"text":"\n"},{"number":1337,"text":"Any material product, application, module, graph function, runtime surface,\n"},{"number":1338,"text":"plugin surface, public interface, data surface, or capability has an operational\n"},{"number":1339,"text":"lifecycle. A requirement can say \"compositional graphs\" and a design ADR can describe Fragment types, but only a scenario asks \"can I model a reusable discovery workflow and apply it twice?\"\n"},{"number":2633,"text":"\n"},{"number":2634,"text":"Scenarios are the product-owner layer. Without an adequate\n"},{"number":2636,"text":"scenario, that layer has not demonstrated the capability. A failed scenario\n"},{"number":2637,"text":"supplies a discrepancy to [Universal Intake Triage](#universal-intake-triage);\n"},{"number":2638,"text":"the failure alone does not distinguish a realization defect, inadequate\n"},{"number":2639,"text":"governing meaning, inaccurate prediction or an inadequate scenario, oracle or\n"},{"number":2640,"text":"observation. They primarily validate capability claims and other behavior with concrete operational meaning. This section owns the Product-level test-authority taxonomy.\n"},{"number":2652,"text":"`DESIGN_MODULE_METHOD.md` retains ownership of module-derived unit-proof law;\n"},{"number":2653,"text":"the shared context does not merge those authority sources.\n"},{"number":2654,"text":"\n"},{"number":2655,"text":"Every executable proof surface must declare its authority source.\n"},{"number":2656,"text":"\n"},{"number":2657,"text":"Given F1, F2, F4–F6 and a claim spanning operational boundaries, a narrower\n"},{"number":2658,"text":"witness cannot establish the composed claim. Those policies admit varied runners and realizations while\n"},{"number":2663,"text":"preserving the exact claim and evidence boundary.\n"},{"number":2664,"text":"\n"},{"number":2665,"text":"For ticketed work, the immediate proof authority is the admitted ticket and its\n"},{"number":2666,"text":"triage path. Direct\n"},{"number":2721,"text":"source-level unit tests, helper tests, and module integration tests may be\n"},{"number":2722,"text":"valuable proof, but they are not UAT and cannot close user acceptance by\n"},{"number":2723,"text":"themselves.\n"},{"number":2724,"text":"\n"},{"number":2725,"text":"Sandbox UAT has two lawful execution modes:\n"},{"number":2726,"text":"\n"},{"number":2727,"text":"- **Harnessed sandbox UAT**: exercises the full composed product path with a\n"},{"number":2728,"text":"  deterministic, fake, recorded, or injected worker/result surface.\n"},{"number":2729,"text":"- **Live sandbox UAT**: exercises the full composed product path with a real\n"},{"number":2730,"text":"  configured worker, tool, agent, service, or other external probabilistic\n"},{"number":2731,"text":"  transport where the product depends on that live boundary.\n"},{"number":2732,"text":"\n"},{"number":2733,"text":"Harnessed sandbox UAT proves composition, orchestration, control, projection,\n"},{"number":2734,"text":"archive, and scenario wiring without relying on live probabilistic execution.\n"},{"number":2735,"text":"It is the right lane for deterministic reproducibility.\n"},{"number":2736,"text":"\n"},{"number":2737,"text":"Live sandbox UAT proves that the same scenario can cross the real external\n"},{"number":2738,"text":"probabilistic boundary and return evidence through the declared result,\n"},{"number":2739,"text":"evaluation, provenance, and projection surfaces. Where a product or release\n"},{"number":2740,"text":"claim depends on live probabilistic compute, semantic green, unit green, module\n"},{"number":2741,"text":"integration green, and harnessed sandbox green are necessary but not sufficient\n"},{"number":2742,"text":"for live acceptance.\n"},{"number":2743,"text":"\n"},{"number":2744,"text":"Terminology rules:\n"},{"number":2745,"text":"\n"},{"number":2746,"text":"- `unit` names module/design conformance scope, not user acceptance.\n"},{"number":2747,"text":"- `integration` names execution breadth, not authority source.\n"},{"number":2748,"text":"- `sandbox` names composed-product acceptance execution.\n"},{"number":2749,"text":"- `harnessed`, `fake`, `recorded`, or `deterministic` name non-live sandbox\n"},{"number":2750,"text":"  execution.\n"},{"number":2751,"text":"- `live` is reserved for real external worker or transport execution. Significant Path Coverage Rule\n"},{"number":2844,"text":"\n"},{"number":2845,"text":"The proving surface must declare and exercise the significant paths for the\n"},{"number":2846,"text":"behavior being claimed.\n"},{"number":2847,"text":"\n"},{"number":2848,"text":"Exhaustive proof does not mean every possible branch in every file. It means the\n"},{"number":2849,"text":"project names the meaningful paths and proves them intentionally.\n"},{"number":2850,"text":"\n"},{"number":2851,"text":"The significant paths usually include, where relevant:\n"},{"number":2852,"text":"\n"},{"number":2853,"text":"- intended success path\n"},{"number":2854,"text":"- fail-closed or rejection path\n"},{"number":2855,"text":"- boundary or control path\n"},{"number":2856,"text":"- integration, dispatch, or transport path\n"},{"number":2857,"text":"- recovery, retry, or replay path\n"},{"number":2858,"text":"\n"},{"number":2859,"text":"If the significant paths are not declared, scenario coverage cannot be judged.\n"},{"number":2860,"text":"If they are declared but not exercised, the behavior is not fully proved.\n"},{"number":2861,"text":"\n"},{"number":2862,"text":"---\n"},{"number":2863,"text":"\n"}]},{"end_line":2888,"heading":"Spec-Driven Homeostatic Methodology/Requirement Categories","id":"repo://stdo-v3-generation-rc9/codex/5592168e26759f37e730aed12c030a6061709ec4/source/standards/SPEC_METHOD.md#Spec-Driven%20Homeostatic%20Methodology%2FRequirement%20Categories","path":"standards/SPEC_METHOD.md","sha256":"bfc29fb5fb2945408e52d77def912541eb35ddcac3bccf1c1faa4282b714a621","start_line":2864,"source_file":"/Users/jim/src/apps/specification_methodology/a_c/dogfood/stdo-v3-generation-rc9/runs-v2/codex/graphify-ac/corpus/standards/SPEC_METHOD.md","lines":[{"number":2864,"text":"## Requirement Categories\n"},{"number":2865,"text":"\n"},{"number":2866,"text":"Every live requirement family shall be categorized by what kind of truth it asserts. This prevents the common mistake of treating the entire requirement surface as if it were only a feature list.\n"},{"number":2867,"text":"\n"},{"number":2868,"text":"| Category | Meaning | Typical question |\n"},{"number":2869,"text":"|----------|---------|------------------|\n"},{"number":2870,"text":"| **Capability** | A behavior, function, or surface the system must provide | \"What can the system do?\" |\n"},{"number":2871,"text":"| **Constraint / Guarantee** | An invariant, law, safety property, portability rule, or semantic guarantee that bounds how the system may behave | \"What must always remain true?\" |\n"},{"number":2872,"text":"| **Governance** | A policy or control rule over lifecycle, authority, visibility, release, or operator action | \"What governs use and change?\" |\n"},{"number":2873,"text":"| **Verification** | A requirement on tests, evidence, traceability, qualification, or forensic surfaces | \"How do we know the claim is true?\" |\n"},{"number":2874,"text":"\n"},{"number":2875,"text":"Requirements remain part of the constitutional layer in all four cases. A category is not a priority label and not a claim that the requirement is user-facing. It is a statement of what kind of project truth the requirement owns.\n"},{"number":2876,"text":"\n"},{"number":2877,"text":"The important distinction is:\n"},{"number":2878,"text":"\n"},{"number":2879,"text":"- Requirements describe the full constitutional **what**.\n"},{"number":2880,"text":"- Design describes the structural **how**. ADRs are one durable design form.\n"},{"number":2881,"text":"- Scenarios validate the operational meaning of capability claims.\n"},{"number":2882,"text":"\n"},{"number":2883,"text":"Therefore, not every requirement should be translated into a marketed \"feature.\" Some requirements are better expressed as guarantees, constraints, governance rules, or verification obligations.\n"},{"number":2884,"text":"\n"},{"number":2885,"text":"Requirements should also avoid freezing concrete realization choices prematurely. Unless a path, filename, or packaging rule is itself part of the constitutional truth, those choices belong to design rather than the requirement surface.\n"},{"number":2886,"text":"\n"},{"number":2887,"text":"---\n"},{"number":2888,"text":"\n"}]},{"end_line":2928,"heading":"Spec-Driven Homeostatic Methodology/Reconstruction Litmus","id":"repo://stdo-v3-generation-rc9/codex/5592168e26759f37e730aed12c030a6061709ec4/source/standards/SPEC_METHOD.md#Spec-Driven%20Homeostatic%20Methodology%2FReconstruction%20Litmus","path":"standards/SPEC_METHOD.md","sha256":"b7280a35572aabe072475244212b192256a8c63f9011564f8164aacbca4b6378","start_line":2889,"source_file":"/Users/jim/src/apps/specification_methodology/a_c/dogfood/stdo-v3-generation-rc9/runs-v2/codex/graphify-ac/corpus/standards/SPEC_METHOD.md","lines":[{"number":2889,"text":"## Reconstruction Litmus\n"},{"number":2890,"text":"\n"},{"number":2891,"text":"The methodology is specification-driven only if the layers are reconstructable in order:\n"},{"number":2892,"text":"\n"},{"number":2893,"text":"1. Given **Goals**, a competent team should be able to derive a conformant **Intent** surface.\n"},{"number":2894,"text":"2. Given **Intent**, a competent team should be able to derive a conformant **Product** definition.\n"},{"number":2895,"text":"3. Given **Product**, a competent team should be able to derive conformant **Requirements**.\n"},{"number":2896,"text":"4. Given **Requirements**, a competent team should be able to derive a conformant **Design**.\n"},{"number":2897,"text":"5. Given **Requirements + Design**, a competent team should be able to derive a conformant **Realization** in its selected execution environment.\n"},{"number":2898,"text":"\n"},{"number":2899,"text":"This does not mean the derivation is unique. Different designs may satisfy the\n"},{"number":2900,"text":"same requirements, and different software, human or mixed realizations may\n"},{"number":2901,"text":"satisfy the same design. The test is sufficiency, not determinism.\n"},{"number":2902,"text":"\n"},{"number":2903,"text":"Failures at any boundary indicate a specification defect:\n"},{"number":2904,"text":"\n"},{"number":2905,"text":"- If intent cannot be derived from goals, the goals surface is unclear, contradictory, or not concrete enough to orient the next bounded wave of work.\n"},{"number":2906,"text":"- If product cannot be derived from intent, the intent surface is too vague, contradictory, or not concrete enough to guide realization.\n"},{"number":2907,"text":"- If requirements cannot be derived from product, the product surface is underspecified, contradictory, or not decomposable enough to govern implementation.\n"},{"number":2908,"text":"- If design cannot be derived from requirements, the requirement surface is underspecified, contradictory, or polluted with accidental implementation detail.\n"},{"number":2909,"text":"- If a realization cannot be derived from requirements plus design, the design surface is incomplete, ambiguous, or not operational enough for its declared binding.\n"},{"number":2910,"text":"\n"},{"number":2911,"text":"ADRs and design documents provide the governing decisions needed to realize the\n"},{"number":2912,"text":"constitutional obligations in the selected environment.\n"},{"number":2913,"text":"\n"},{"number":2914,"text":"Reconstruction sufficiency also depends on ambiguity governance. If a boundary\n"},{"number":2915,"text":"can be crossed only by hiding a major unresolved ambiguity, the upstream surface\n"},{"number":2916,"text":"is not yet sufficient even if some downstream artifact can be produced.\n"},{"number":2917,"text":"\n"},{"number":2918,"text":"For an already accepted relation, reconstruction may reuse its exact current\n"},{"number":2919,"text":"basis and sufficient evidence after checking applicability and invalidation.\n"},{"number":2920,"text":"Reconstructability requires a correct reconstruction route, not its execution\n"},{"number":2921,"text":"at every use. It grants no restart authority and does not excuse stale success.\n"},{"number":2922,"text":"The claim determines the reconstruction span; a local selected function does\n"},{"number":2923,"text":"not require recreating the whole Product. New material counterexamples still\n"},{"number":2924,"text":"challenge the affected relation. Where `DESIGN_MODULE_METHOD.md` applies, it\n"},{"number":2925,"text":"owns design-view coverage, its material-change gate and unchanged-boundary\n"},{"number":2926,"text":"reuse; this litmus does not waive that gate or require recreating unchanged\n"},{"number":2927,"text":"accepted views.\n"},{"number":2928,"text":"\n"}]},{"end_line":2947,"heading":"Spec-Driven Homeostatic Methodology/Design decides the concrete mechanism that realizes them.\n"},{"number":2934,"text":"\n"},{"number":2935,"text":"If a technology stack, carrier, or runtime is not already constrained by an\n"},{"number":2936,"text":"upstream constitutional surface, choosing it is part of design rather than a\n"},{"number":2937,"text":"separate upstream authority.\n"},{"number":2938,"text":"\n"},{"number":2939,"text":"That boundary matters especially for agent-facing bootstrap and control topology\n"},{"number":2943,"text":"\n"},{"number":2944,"text":"If filenames and placement are hardcoded in requirements without constitutional necessity, the requirement surface has absorbed design
